L434: ## 7.2.1.6 Patient Reported Outcomes (PROs)
L435: The protocols specified three secondary efficacy endpoints based on patient reported outcomes (PROs). One of these endpoints was based on the Dermatology Life Quality Index (DLQI). In an advice letter dated February 19, 2016, the Agency stated that the clinical relevance of DLQI endpoint would require COA input.
L436: The other two PRO endpoints were based on the Psoriasis Symptom Scale (PSS):
L437: - PSS of 0 at Week 16 (risankizumab vs. placebo)
- Change in PSS score from baseline to Week 16 (risankizumab vs. ustekinumab).
L438: At a Type C Guidance Meeting dated April 5, 2017, the Agency stated that “we do not have sufficient information to determine whether the PSS is a well-defined and reliable
L439: instrument that may be used to support labeling claims". The Agency requested that the sponsor "provide evidence of the assessment's content validity and other measurement properties, including reliability, validity, and ability to detect change, as well as the assessment's conceptual framework and scoring in a PRO evidence dossier for FDA review and comment". Furthermore, the Agency commented on the limited utility of interpreting the results of a van Elteren non-parametric test, as a mere change in PSS might not reflect a clinically meaningful change.
L440: On July 16, 2018, the clinical team requested a Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Consult regarding the proposed PSS. The COA reviewer (Julia Ju, PharmD, PhD) concluded in the COA review (January 23, 2019) that "the evidence submitted by the applicant sufficiently demonstrates that PSS is fit-for-purpose for the context of this drug development program to assess the signs and symptoms of psoriasis".
L441: For the DLQI, the Study Endpoints and Label Development (SEALD) reviews (October 13, 2004; May 20, 2005) concluded that the DLQI is not fit-for-purpose for labeling in the context of this drug development program due to lack of item relevancy. The DLQI contains questions that measure broad concepts related to general skin conditions (e.g., going shopping, making your home messy) which may lack clinical relevance and be insensitive to treatment effect among [dermatological condition under investigation] patients. Further, there are issues surrounding content validity, specifically the use of multi-barreled questions in the assessment.
L442: Table 12 includes the results for the DLQI endpoints as well as the mean change from baseline in PSS score to Week 16. In both trials, risankizumab was statistically superior to ustekinumab for the DLQI endpoint (p-values&lt;0.001), and against placebo for the PSS endpoints (p-values&lt;0.001)..
L443: *CDTL comment:*
*COA found the PSS but not the DLQI fit for purpose as they relate to inclusion in labeling. PSS information is included in labeling.*

L461: ## 7.2.1.8.2 Center
L462: Trial 1311.28 was conducted in 65 centers. Note that Trial 1311.28 excluded Dr. Woodson's site. It should be noted that Dr. Woodson's site enrolled a total of 11 subjects, and given the relative large treatment effect, the inclusion or exclusion for such site did not impact the conclusion of the efficacy. L478: Based on the inspection by the Office of Scientific Inspections (OSI), approximately 0.4%, 3.1%, and 1.8% in Trials M15-995 (1311.28), M16-008 (1311.3), and M15-992 (1311.4), respectively, did not have a clearly attributable signature of a certified assessor to verify the accuracy of the baseline and/or Week 16 sPGA data. Similarly, for the PASI score, approximately 1.6%, 4.3%, and 5.3% in Trials 1311.28, 1311.3, and 1311.4, respectively, did not have a clearly attributable signature. However, given the large treatment effect (approximately 80% in all 3 trials) along with a relatively small proportion of subjects without the attributable signature, results from a sensitivity analysis of excluding these subjects would not impact the overall conclusion.
L479: ## 7.2.2. Other Phase 3 Trials (M16-010 [1311.30] and M15-992 [1311.4])
L480: ## 7.2.2.1 Study Design and Endpoints
L481: **Trial 1311.30** was an active-controlled, double-blind, double-dummy, randomized, parallel design to test superiority of BI 655066 vs. adalimumab with treatment duration of 44 weeks.
L482: Using the Interactive Response Technology (IRT), 605 subjects from 66 centers were randomized to the following treatment arms in a 1:1 ratio:
L483: - Risankizumab (301 subjects)
- Adalimumab (304 subjects)
L484: In this trial, subjects initially randomized to BI 655066 remained on BI 655066. Then subjects randomized to adalimumab were rerandomized to the following treatment depending on their PASI score at Week 16:
L485: - subjects with &lt; PASI 50 at Week 16 switched to the BI 655066
- subjects with ≥ PASI 90 at Week 16 stayed on adalimumab
- subjects with ≥ PASI 50 and &lt; PASI 90 at Week 16 were re-randomized 1:1 to receive either BI 655066 or adalimumab.
L486: The Agency previously stated (June 24, 2015, April 5, 2017) that “As treatment success for Humira was based on PASI 75 and PGA 0 or 1 endpoints, your re-randomization based on PASI 50 and PASI 90 (i.e., PASI50 but &lt;PASI 90) may be arbitrary taking into account that your sub-population for PASI 50 but &lt;PASI 90 could be further partitioned to those who achieve PASI 75 and those who do not. Therefore, we reiterate our previous comments (6/24/2015) that you define success criteria on PASI 75 as well as PGA 0 or 1 at Week 16.”
L487: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L488: Furthermore, in an Advice Letter dated 3/9/2016, the Agency stated that “In general, to support a superiority claim against an active comparator, replication of study findings from two adequate and well-controlled trials is recommended”.
L489: To maintain double-blind, the sponsor used a double-dummy design as summarized below.
L490: - **BI 655066 150 mg SC**BI 655066 - 2 syringes of active drug (2x75 mg) at randomization, Weeks 4, 16, and 28,
- Placebo adalimumab – 2 syringes at randomization and 1 syringe Q2W from Weeks 1 to Week 41
- **Adalimumab**Adalimumab – 2 syringes of adalimumab at randomization (2x40 mg) followed by 1 syringe adalimumab (40 mg) Q2W from Weeks 1 to 41
- Placebo BI 655066 – 2 syringes of placebo BI 655066 at randomization, Weeks 4, 16, 20, 28, and 32.
L491: At Week 16, subjects that switched to the **BI 655066** received the following:
L492: - BI 655066 – 2 syringes at randomization, Week 4 followed by 2 injections (2x75 mg) at Weeks 16, 20, and 32
- Placebo Adalimumab Q2W from Weeks 17 to 41.
L493: At Week 16, subjects that continued on **adalimumab** received the following:
L494: - Adalimumab - 1 syringe of adalimumab (40 mg) Q2W from Weeks 17 to 41
- Placebo BI 655066 – 2 syringes at Week 28.
L495: Study visits occurred on screening (Day -28), baseline (Day 1), Weeks 1, 3, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44 (end of treatment), and follow-up.
L496: **Figure 8. Study Design for Trial 1311.30**
L497: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L498: Source: applicant's protocol
L499: For Trial 1311.30, the protocol-specified co-primary endpoints were the following:
L500: - Proportion of subjects achieving sPGA 0 or 1 at Week 16
- Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 90 at Week 16.
L501: The protocol specified testing the following secondary efficacy endpoints in the following:
L502: - Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 75 at Week 16
- Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 100 at Week 16.
L503: Then for the re-randomized subjects at Week 16, the protocol-specified testing the superiority of BI 655066 to placebo for the following endpoint.
L504: - Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 90 at Week 44 (for those who were re-randomized at Week 16).
L505: For **Trial 1311.4**, a total of 507 subjects from 60 centers were enrolled and randomized to one of the following treatment arms in a 4:1 ratio:
L506: - BI 655066 150 mg SC at Weeks 0, 4 and 16 (407 subjects)
- Placebo SC at Weeks 0 and 4, then BI 655066 150 mg SC at Week 16 (100 subjects)
L507: As with the pivotal trials (1311.28 and 1311.3), randomization was stratified by weight (\le 100 kg vs. &gt;100 kg), and prior exposure to TNF antagonists (0 vs. \ge 1).
L508: There were two parts included in this trial: Part A and Part B.
L509: - Part A: "induction and response period from initial randomization through Week 28"
- Part B: "withdrawal and re-treatment period from Weeks 28-88".
L510: The protocol included a figure depicting the trial design as in Figure 9.
L511: At Week 28,
L512: - “Non-responders” (i.e., sPGA\ge2) received BI 655066 open-label until Week 88 regardless of the original treatment arm in Part A.
- “Responders” (i.e., sPGA 0 or 1) continued to receive the blinded drug, according to the following criteria:Subjects originally randomized to the active were re-randomized to either BI 655066 or placebo in a 1:2 ratio.
- Subjects originally randomized to placebo continued to receive blinded BI 655066 treatment to maintain blinding.
L513: Once a Week 28 responder subject reached sPGA \ge 3, they switched over to open-label BI 655066.
L514: Subjects were evaluated at the following study visits: screening (Day -28), baseline (Day 1), Weeks 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52, 58, 64, 70, 76, 88 (end of treatment), 94, 104 (follow-ups).
L515: The trial design is shown below.
L516: **Figure 9. Study Design for Trial 1311.4**
L517: Source: applicant's protocol
L518: The protocol specified testing superiority of BI 655066 to placebo in the following secondary efficacy endpoints in the following order:
L519: - Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 75 at Week 16
- Proportion of subjects achieving PASI 100 at Week 16
- Proportion of subjects achieving sPGA of 0 at Week 16
L520: - Proportion of subjects achieving DLQI 0 or 1 at Week 16
L521: For the re-randomized subjects at Week 28, the sponsor included an endpoint of comparing BI 655066 vs. placebo sPGA 0 or 1 at Week 52.
L522: ## 7.2.2.2 Statistical Methodologies
L523: The primary analysis population was the Intent to Treat (ITT) population defined as all randomized subjects. The SAPs specified conducting supportive analyses using the Per Protocol Set (PPS) defined as the following:
L524: - Subjects who received at least 75% of planned study drug injections (including dummy placebo injections).
- Subjects who had either a PASI or sPGA assessment post-baseline
- Subjects who had stable moderate to severe chronic plaque psoriasis at baseline (BSA\ge10%, PASI\ge12, sPGA\ge3)
- Subjects who had at least one post-baseline assessment for the corresponding endpoint.
L525: For the analysis of the co-primary endpoints and the binary secondary endpoints, the protocols specified using the Cochran Mantel Haenszel (CMH) test stratified by weight (\le100 kg vs. &gt;100 kg) and priori exposure to TNF antagonists (0 vs \ge1).
L526: The protocols specified testing the endpoints in a hierarchical order to control the Type I error rate (two-sided, \alpha=0.05), and only if the two co-primary endpoints are statistically significant, the secondary endpoints would be tested.
L527: For handling of missing data, the protocols specified using the same methods as in the pivotal trials.

The Agency stated that “we anticipate inclusion in labeling of descriptive study results related to withdrawal/retreatment”.

L585: ## 7.3. Review of Safety
L586: ## 7.3.1. Safety Review Approach
L587: The primary review of safety of risankizumab for the treatment of moderate to severe plaque psoriasis focused on the evaluation of pooled data from Phase 2 Trial 1311.2 and Phase 3 Trials M15-995 (1311.28), M16-008 (1311.3), M16-010 (1311.30) and M15-992 (1311.4).
L588: The Phase 2 Trial 1311.2 was a multicenter, randomized, dose-ranging, active-control, double-blind (within dose groups of risankizumab) evaluation of risankizumab 90 mg or 180 mg subcutaneous (SC) at Weeks 0, 4, and q12w thereafter or 18 mg at Week 0 in adults with stable moderate to severe chronic plaque psoriasis psoriasis \ge 6 months (with or without psoriatic arthritis); body surface area (BSA) involvement \ge 10%; Psoriasis Area and Severity Index (PASI) score \ge 12; Static Physician Global Assessment (sPGA) \ge 3; and candidates for systemic therapy or phototherapy.
L589: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L590: The 4 pivotal studies were multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo and/or active comparator-controlled evaluations of the proposed dose and dosing regimen (risankizumab 150 mg subcutaneous (SC) at Weeks 0, 4, and q12w thereafter) in adults with stable moderate to severe chronic plaque psoriasis \ge 6 months (with or without psoriatic arthritis); body surface area (BSA) involvement \ge 10%; Psoriasis Area and Severity Index (PASI) score \ge 12; Static Physician Global Assessment (sPGA) \ge 3; and candidates for systemic therapy or phototherapy. These studies include:
L591: Study M15-995 (1311.28) (UltIMMa-2): Multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled studies that compared the efficacy and safety of risankizumab treatment with placebo and ustekinumab treatment
L592: Study M16-008 (1311.3) (UltIMMa-1): Multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled studies that compared the efficacy and safety of risankizumab treatment with placebo and ustekinumab treatment
L593: Study M15-992 (1311.4) (IMMhance): A multicenter, randomized, doubleblind study that compared the efficacy and safety of risankizumab treatment with placebo, evaluated the effects of continuous risankizumab compared with withdrawal in risankizumab responders, and evaluated re-treatment of subjects who experienced relapse
L594: Study M16-010 (1311.30) (IMMvent): A multicenter, randomized, doubleblind study that compared the efficacy and safety of risankizumab with adalimumab and evaluated switching from adalimumab to risankizumab for adalimumab non-responders and those with inadequate response to adalimumab
L595: The Long-Term Safety Extension Additional trials to inform safety include the following:
L596: - A long-term, open-label extension (OLE) study (Study M15-997) that is planned for 154 weeks and is currently ongoing. Subjects from lead-in Phase 3 studies are dosed subcutaneously at 150 mg of risankizumab every 12 weeks.
- A long-term OLE (Study 1311.13) that is planned for 4 years and is currently ongoing. Subjects from the lead-in Phase 2 study are dosed subcutaneously at 90 mg or 180 mg of risankizumab every 12 weeks.
L597: To assess the safety of risankizumab across clinical studies, subject data were integrated into three integrated analysis sets:
L598: - Primary Safety Pool provides data regarding the short-term (16 week) safety
- Ustekinumab (EU sourced) Controlled provides data regarding the long-term (52 week) safety
- All Risankizumab-Psoriasis provides additional open-label long-term safety

L605: The applicant did not establish an adequate scientific bridge between U.S. licensed Stelara and EU approved ustekinumab(b) (4)
L606: Discussion regarding the comparative efficacy and safety of the proposed product with the ustekinumab is minimized in this review.
L607: The review team analyzed the following types of pooled data: exposure, demographics and baseline characteristics, prior therapies for psoriasis, treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs), serious AEs (SAEs), and AEs leading to discontinuation. The applicant identified some adverse events of special interest which included severe infections, malignancies, non-melanoma skin cancer, melanoma skin cancer, adjudicated cardiovascular (CV) events (including major adverse cardiovascular events [MACE]), hepatic events, suicide ideation and behavior, injection site reactions and drug related hypersensitivity reactions. Also, the review team evaluated the impact of anti-drug antibodies on the adverse event profile. Refer to Section 6.1 and the Clinical Pharmacology Appendix.
L608: ## 7.3.2. Review of the Safety Database
L609: ## Overall Exposure
L610: In the Phase 2 and Phase 3 risankizumab clinical studies in subjects with plaque psoriasis, 2,234 subjects (2,166.6 patient-years [PY]) were exposed to at least 1 dose of risankizumab (18, 90, 150, or 180 mg), with 1,208 (54.1%) subjects exposed to risankizumab for \ge 1 year, 96 (4.3%) subjects exposed \ge 2 years, and 30 (1.3%) subjects exposed \ge 3 years. Of the total 2,234 subjects, 1,590 subjects received \ge 1 dose of risankizumab 150 mg with a total of 1,688.0 PY of exposure. A total of 1,091 subjects received at least 1 dose of risankizumab 150 mg for \ge 1 year. Ninety-six subjects had \ge 2 years of risankizumab exposure at any dosage level. Thirty subjects had \ge 3 years of risankizumab exposure at any dosage level.
L611: The size of the overall safety database was considered adequate and consistent with ICH E1.
L612: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L613: Demographic characteristics of the subject population in the risankizumab development program are presented in greater detail in Section 7.2.1.3 of this review. The risankizumab psoriasis clinical study population was predominantly male, white, and tumor necrosis factor (TNF) naïve; the average subject was mid-forties in age and weighed approximately 90 kg. Across analysis sets, approximately 10% of subjects were between 65 and 74 years of age and approximately 1% were 75 years of age or older.
L614: The population appears to be representative of the US target population based on age and race. Prevalence of psoriasis is considered to be balanced between the sexes. However, several studies have indicated sex differences in the severity of disease, with women having less severe psoriasis.
L615: Demographic information that was collected was generally balanced across the treatment arms, including history of psoriatic arthritis and cardiovascular disease,
L616: cardiovascular risk factors and prior therapy.
L617: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L618: The total exposure to risankizumab, 150 mg SC at Week 0 and 4, then every 12 weeks thereafter for 52 weeks, provides adequate data for the evaluation of safety. The demographics of the study population are sufficiently representative of the target population. Therefore, the safety database presented by the applicant is sufficient to characterize the safety profile of risankizumab for the treatment of moderate to severe plaque psoriasis in patients who are candidates for systemic therapy.
L619: ## 7.3.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L620: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L621: Overall, the quality of the data submitted is adequate to characterize the safety and efficacy of risankizumab. We discovered no significant deficiencies that would impede a thorough analysis of the data presented by the applicant.
L622: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L623: Adverse events were coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 20. Each AE was coded to a system organ class (SOC) and preferred term (PT). The coding of adverse events in the BLA submission appeared adequate and allowed for accurate estimation of AE risks.
L624: Treatment-emergent AEs (TEAEs) were defined by the applicant as events with onset after the first dose of study drug and no more than 105 days (i.e., 5 times the half-life of risankizumab) after the last dose of study drug. If an incomplete onset date was collected for an AE, the AE was assumed to be treatment-emergent unless there was evidence that confirmed that the AE was not treatment-emergent (e.g., the AE end date was prior to the date of the first dose of study drug). Exposure-adjusted TEAE rates were calculated as events/100 PY. For this calculation, 1 year was considered to be 365.25 days.
L625: The definition of AE, TEAE, and SAE are acceptable. The classification system used by investigators to describe the severity of AE as well as the causal relationship between AE and study product are also acceptable. The applicant's identification and presentation of AE of special interest was appropriate.
L626: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L627: Safety analyses included reporting of AEs, laboratory (screening for tuberculosis, screening for pregnancy, hematology, clinical chemistry, and urinalysis), physical examination (including vital signs and 12-lead electrocardiogram), and local tolerability. The severity of the laboratory test values was assessed using CTCAE version 4.0. The applicant conducted evaluations of mean changes over time and individual subject
L628: changes in hematology and clinical chemistry values, as well as potentially clinically significant abnormalities in hematology and clinical chemistry.
L629: Safety monitoring and the applicant's evaluation of routine clinical tests are appropriate.
L630: ## 7.3.4. Safety Results
L631: ## Deaths
L632: In the overall development plan, deaths occurred in 5 subjects treated with risankizumab and 2 subjects treated with adalimumab. There were no deaths in the clinical studies conducted in other (non-psoriasis) indications. Of the 5 deaths in risankizumab-treated subjects, 4 occurred within 105 days after the last dose of risankizumab. Of the 5 deaths, 2 were adjudicated as cardiac deaths (acute myocardial infarction and sudden cardiac death), 2 were due to unknown cause, and 1 was a cancer death. Of the 2 deaths in adalimumab-treated subjects 1 was cancer (gallbladder) and 1 was sepsis (post-operative). Summaries of deaths in risankizumab-treated subjects are as follows:
L633: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) in Trial M16-010 is a 47-year-old, white male with a history of pre-existing MI, PCI with stent placement (approximately a year prior), obesity, diabetes type II, hypertension, chronic heart failure (NYHA II), dyslipidemia, cardiac hypertrophy, atrial fibrillation, treated with risankizumab 150 mg for psoriasis/ psoriatic arthritis. On Day 73 (9 days after last dose) the subject died at home due to an acute myocardial infarction. Autopsy confirmed that the cause of death was acute myocardial infarction.
L634: The subject had multiple risk factors for AMI which are likely to be contributory to the cause of death. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L635: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) in Trial M15-995 is a 57-year-old, white male with a history of angina pectoris, diabetes mellitus, hyperlipidemia, hypertension, obesity, coronary artery disease, COPD, left bundle branch block, sleep apnea, and hypertension treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. On Day 189 (161 days after last dose) the subject died of an unknown cause. No details were provided regarding his death. The subject experienced significant cardiovascular AEs earlier in the course of treatment. On Day 46, the subject experienced angina and was hospitalized for worsening of coronary artery disease (CAD) and moderate congestive heart failure. A cardiac catheterization revealed small vessel disease and percutaneous transluminal coronary angioplasty was performed, resolving the subject's chest pain.
L636: The subject had multiple risk factors for sudden death which are likely to be contributory to the cause of death. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L637: 3. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-995 is a 32-year-old, white male with a history of depression, fibromyalgia, chronic pain, tobacco use (3 packs per day), alcohol use, hypertension and obesity treated with risankizumab 150 mg for psoriasis/psoriatic arthritis. Concomitant medications include oxycodone, amitriptyline, cyclobenzaprine HCl, and lorazepam. On Day 385 (101 days after last dose) the subject experienced a seizure, became unresponsive and pulseless, and was unable to be resuscitated. It was later determined that cause of death was due to a drug overdose (cocaine and oxycodone).
L638: It is not reported if the overdose was intentional or unintentional. The subject had multiple risk factors that could increase his chances for potential drug abuse. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L639: 4. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-992 is a 60-year-old, white male with a history of hypertension, morbid obesity, diabetes, sleep apnea and hypercholesterolemia and possible MI treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. On Day 263 (66 days after last dose) the subject died of an unknown cause. No details were provided regarding his death. The subject experienced significant cardiovascular AEs earlier in the course of treatment. On Day 147 the subject experienced a non-ST segment elevation and was hospitalized with congestive heart failure MI. Cardiac catheterization revealed triple vessel disease and a coronary arterial stent was inserted. On Day 166 the subject experienced unstable angina which was treated medically. Study drug was continued until Day 197.
L640: The subject had multiple risk factors for sudden death which are more likely to be contributory to the cause of death than the study drug.
L641: 5. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-992 is a 70-year-old, white male with a history of obesity, diabetes, atrial fibrillation, cardiomyopathy, left bundle branch block, hypothyroidism and mitral valve regurgitation treated with risankizumab 150 mg for psoriasis/psoriatic arthritis. On Day 317 (Post-Treatment Day 33), the subject was hospitalized with abdominal pain, melanotic stool, hematuria and dehydration. The subject was diagnosed with multiple hepatic lesions, pulmonary nodules, ascites and plural effusion. Biopsy determined adenocarcinoma and the subject was discharged on palliative care. On Day 224 (55 days after last dose) the subject died of metastatic intestinal adenocarcinoma.
L642: The subject was on study drug for 10 months prior to diagnosis of metastatic intestinal adenocarcinoma. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L643: With the 120 Day safety update, 5 new deaths were reported, all of which occurred in psoriasis studies in subjects treated with risankizumab. Summaries of deaths are as follows:
L644: 1. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-992 is a 37-year-old, white male with a history of asthma and epilepsy treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. On Day 496 (45 days after last dose) the subject experienced an epileptic seizure and died despite attempts of cardiopulmonary resuscitation. Autopsy reported a sudden grand mal seizure as the cause of death. Toxicology of the coronal matter showed tetrahydrocannabinol. Toxicology screening analysis also detected cannabinoids, caffeine, and nicotine.
L645: The subject had an underlying disease that was likely the major contributing factor leading to his death.
L646: 2. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-992 is a 74-year-old, white male with a history of hypertension, hyperlipidemia, atrial fibrillation, chronic kidney disease, and coronary artery disease status post grafting treated with risankizumab 150 mg for psoriasis/ psoriatic arthritis. On Day 583 (127 days after last dose) the subject died of an unknown cause. No details were provided regarding his death. The subject experienced a significant cardiovascular AE earlier in the course of treatment. On Day 491 (45 days after last treatment) the subject experienced an acute left hemispheric ischemic stroke after laparoscopic cholecystectomy for chronic cholecystitis and cholelithiasis. Carotid Doppler showed complete left internal carotid artery occlusion and right internal carotid artery with more than 50% occlusion. The subject was eventually transferred to a nursing facility for rehabilitation.
L647: The subject had multiple risk factors for sudden death which are more likely to be contributory to the cause of death than the study drug.
L648: 3. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-997 is a 57-year-old, white male with a history of hypertension, obesity, tobacco use (0.1 pack per day) and alcohol use (2-4 drinks per day) treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. On Day 98 the subject sought medical attention for abdominal pain and vomiting. On Day 99, ultrasound of the upper abdomen revealed acute cholecystolithiasis with small pericholecystic collection at the fundus level (rule out probable rupture) and scarce free liquid in Morrison pouch. Evaluation by general surgery was recommended. The subject died in his home on Day 99. The main cause of death on the death certificate was stated as pancreatitis. An autopsy was not performed.
L649: The acute medical issue was likely to be contributory to the cause of death. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L650: 4. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-997 is a 41-year-old, white male with a history of asthma, hypertension, obesity, diabetes, and tobacco use (1 pack per day) treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. The subject had been randomized to adalimumab in prior feeder study. On Day 207 (40 days after last dose) the subject was found unresponsive after complaining of shoulder pain. Cardiopulmonary resuscitation was started, and emergency medical services were called. Resuscitation was unsuccessful and death was pronounced at the hospital.
L651: The subject had multiple risk factors for sudden cardiac death which are likely contributory to the cause of death. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L652: 5. Subject (b) (6) in Trial M15-997 is a 64-year-old, white male with a history of asthma, congestive heart failure (CHF), hypertension, coronary artery disease, atrial fibrillation, and former tobacco use (stopped in 2017, 30 pack year) treated with risankizumab 150 mg for chronic plaque psoriasis. On Day 663 (88 days after last dose) the subject died of an unknown cause. No details were provided regarding his death. The subject experienced significant AEs earlier in the course of treatment. On Day 575, alanine aminotransferase and bilirubin elevations reached criteria for Hy's Law. On Day 608 the subject experienced a worsening of CHF and was hospitalized.
L653: The Investigator considered the event of worsening congestive heart failure as having a reasonable possibility of being related to study drug. The contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L654: ## Serious Adverse Events
L655: The majority of Serious adverse events (SAEs) observed were consistent with those anticipated for a population of patients with moderate to severe psoriasis treated with an immunosuppressant biologic. Overall, infections and cardiac disorders were the most common SAEs reported. These AEs along with other AEs of interest are discussed further in the sections below.
L656: Over the initial 16 weeks, 31 (2.4%) subjects in the risankizumab group had SAEs compared to 12 (4.0%) subjects in the placebo group, 12 (5.0%) subjects in the ustekinumab group and 9 (3.0%) subjects in the adalimumab group. Serious AEs reported in more than 1 risankizumab-treated subject were cellulitis and squamous cell carcinoma of skin (2 subjects each).
L657: SAEs in the risankizumab group leading to discontinuation of study drug were congestive cardiac failure, drug-induced liver injury, liver injury, invasive lobular breast carcinoma, and esophageal carcinoma. The SAEs assessed by the investigators as
L658: possibly related to study drug were drug-induced liver injury, liver injury, cellulitis, herpes zoster, osteomyelitis, and asthma.
L659: After 52 weeks of exposure, 42 (7.0%) subjects in the risankizumab group had SAEs compared to 18 (9.0%) subjects in the ustekinumab group. SAEs reported by more than 1 risankizumab-treated subject were coronary artery disease/ angina and cardiac failure congestive/ left ventricular failure (3 subjects each; 0.5%), and cellulitis, pneumonia, sepsis, basal cell carcinoma, enteritis/gastroenteritis and abortion spontaneous (2 subjects each; 0.3%). In this analysis set, 8 (1.3%) subjects in the risankizumab group had SAEs in the cardiac disorder SOC compared to 1 (0.5%) subject in the ustekinumab group.
L660: Overall, in the All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set, 139 (6.2%) (9.8 E/100 PY) subjects had at least 1 SAE. The most frequently reported SAEs (\ge 3 risankizumab subjects) were basal cell carcinoma, sepsis, cellulitis, pneumonia, coronary artery disease, transient ischemic attack, squamous cell carcinoma of skin, cardiac failure congestive, and myocardial infarction. Infections and infestations was the SOC with the highest number of subjects with SAEs.
L661: In the safety update, 8.3% (9.3 E/100 PY) of subjects had at least 1 SAE. New SAEs reported by \ge 3 subjects in this SUR (cumulative of initial submission and SUR) were acute myocardial infarction (MI), breast cancer, cholecystitis acute, cholelithiasis, depression, erysipelas, fall, ischaemic stroke, osteoarthritis, prostate cancer, and syncope. Infections and infestations continued to be the system order class (SOC) with the highest number of subjects with SAEs.
L662: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L663: In the Primary Safety Pool, at 16 weeks, the proportion of subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg treatment group (0.7%; 9 subjects) who experienced TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study drug was higher than discontinuations in the placebo group (3.0%; 9 subjects)). No TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study drug were reported in more than 1 risankizumab-treated subject. In risankizumab-treated subjects, the AEs leading to discontinuation of study drug that were also SAEs were cardiac failure congestive, drug-induced liver injury, liver injury, invasive lobular breast carcinoma, and esophageal carcinoma. These AEs are discussed further in section 7.3.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues.
L664: At 52 weeks, the AEs leading to the discontinuation of risankizumab treatment in 5 subjects (0.8%) included cardiac failure congestive, edema peripheral, drug-induced liver injury, pneumonia, and vasculitis.
L665: In the All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set, 35 subjects (1.6%) discontinued due to an adverse event. The Infections and infestations, Investigations (the most frequent
L666: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L667: AE was abnormal liver function tests), and Neoplasms benign, malignant and unspecified (the most frequent AE was breast cancer) SOCs had the most subjects with AEs leading to discontinuation of study drug with 7 (0.3%) subjects in each. The AEs leading to discontinuation of study drug in more than 1 subject were pneumonia, ALT increased, and psoriasis (2 subjects each).
L668: Few subjects discontinued by either the 16-week (9 [0.7%] subjects) or 52-week timepoint (5 [0.8%] subjects). Malignancy, infections, hepatic and cardiac events are discussed further in section 7.3.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues.
L669: ## Significant Adverse Events
L670: Refer to Section 7.3.5 “Analysis of Submission-specific Safety Issues”.
L671: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L672: Treatment-emergent AEs (TEAEs) are defined as events with onset after the first dose of study drug (including same as day as study drug start day) and no more than 105 days (i.e., 5 times the half-life of risankizumab) after the last dose of study drug. AEs with an onset during protocol-designed treatment gaps (i.e., placebo period) that occurred more than 105 days from the last dose of the previous risankizumab treatment and before the first dose of retreatment for this study were excluded from TEAE summaries.
L673: Adverse events were summarized as both subject incidence rates and exposure-adjusted event rates per 100 PYs. The former provided the basis of assessment for the 16 week Primary Safety Pool while the latter provided for a more adequate assessment of the long term safety data given the varied duration of exposure across different treatment groups. The exposure adjusted rates also enabled comparison of data across the short-term and long-term analysis sets.
L674: In the first 16 weeks of treatment, 638 (48.9%) subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg group experienced at least 1 AE, which was comparable with placebo in which 145 subjects (48.3%) experienced an AE. The following events occurred in at least 2% of subjects in the 150 mg risankizumab group: upper respiratory tract infections, viral upper respiratory tract infections, headache, arthralgia, and fatigue; of these, arthralgia occurred at a numerically higher rate in the placebo group compared to the risankizumab group.
L675: Upper respiratory infections (URI) are common illnesses and comprise the two most frequent preferred terms upper respiratory tract infection and viral upper respiratory tract infection. These terms may be pooled for labeling. Other common infections (urinary tract infection and gastroenteritis) are reported as common AEs. Infections are discussed in section 7.3.5 and product labeling for infections is recommended. Common adverse events reported in the primary safety pool are shown in the Table 25.

L683: Over the entire psoriasis program, 1480 (66.2%) subjects experienced 5143 adverse events (236.0 E/100 PY). The exposure-adjusted rates appear to slightly increase over time compared to the 52 week timepoint. The type of common AE (by preferred term) and the percentage of subjects experiencing a given AE were comparable to the 52 week timepoint.
L684: ## Laboratory Findings
L685: Evaluation of mean changes over time and individual subject changes in hematology and clinical chemistry values, as well as potentially clinically significant abnormalities in hematology and clinical chemistry, did not identify any significant safety concerns with risankizumab treatment. No special laboratory monitoring is required for safety monitoring with risankizumab therapy.
L686: ## Vital Signs
L687: There were no clinically meaningful changes in vital sign parameters (blood pressure, respiratory rate, pulse rate, or temperature in the primary safety pool. Additionally, there were no clinically meaningful changes in vital signs reported in the safety update reporting period.
L688: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L689: ECG changes (such as absolute and interval changes in QTc) were not meaningfully different across treatment groups. Evaluation of mean changes over time and individual subject changes in ECG data did not reveal any significant safety issue.
L690: ## QT
L691: The ICH E14 guideline regarding the clinical evaluation of QT/QTc interval prolongation and proarrhythmic potential for nonantiarrhythmic drugs does not specifically address QT assessments for biologic agents. Large proteins and monoclonal antibodies have a low likelihood of direct ion channel interactions. The Agency finds the applicant's request to waive thorough QT (TQT) acceptable based on large size of monoclonal antibody risankizumab. Although a thorough QT study was not performed for risankizumab, ECG monitoring was conducted in the Phase 3 trials. In the Primary Safety Pool, ECG changes (such as absolute and interval changes in QTc) were not meaningfully different across treatment groups.
L692: ## Immunogenicity
L693: Because therapeutic proteins have the potential to elicit an immune response, immunogenicity was assessed. Refer to Section 6.2.4.1 for immunogenicity findings. Overall, there was little clinically meaningful impact due to immunogenicity.
L694: Among 1288 evaluable subjects in the Phase 3 studies, approximately 19% of subjects treated with risankizumab developed ADA by Week 16. Of the subjects who developed ADA, approximately 42% (8% of all subjects) had neutralizing antibodies (NAb). The ADA and NAb incidences were 24% and 57% (14% of all subjects), respectively, among 1079 evaluable subjects by Week 52. The findings of ADA impact on bioavailability, efficacy and safety are as follows:
L695: - Antibodies to risankizumab did not appear to affect risankizumab concentrations when ADA titer values were &lt;128. The mean risankizumab concentrations in subjects who had ADA titer values \ge128 were approximately 30% lower compared with the concentrations in ADA negative subjects.
- The development of ADA with antibody titer values \ge128 was associated with reduced efficacy.
- Subjects who developed antibodies to risankizumab were more likely to experience injection-site reactions compared to subjects who were ADA negative.
L696: ## 7.3.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L697: Some categories of AEs were considered to be areas of safety interest due to their higher prevalence in the moderate to severe psoriasis population, concerns with injected immunoglobulin products, or concerns with the immunomodulatory effect of the drug.
L698: The AEs in areas of safety interest, which are discussed in detail include:
L699: - infections (including serious infections, tuberculosis (TB), opportunistic infections, fungal infections, and herpes zoster)
L700: - malignancies (including all possible malignancies, malignant tumors, nonmelanoma skin cancer [NMSC], and malignant tumors excluding NMSC)
- cardiovascular (CV) events (including major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE), extended MACE, and other CV events)
- hypersensitivity and anaphylactic reactions
- injection-site reactions
- neuropsychiatric events [including depression and suicidal ideation and behavior (SIB)]
- hepatic events
L701: ## Infections
L702: Infections (including serious infections, TB, opportunistic infections, fungal infections, and herpes zoster) are considered to be an area of safety interest for risankizumab. The applicant used a query with a more comprehensive list of PTs from both the infections and infestations SOC and the investigations SOC for the evaluation of opportunistic infections. The analysis appears acceptable and the applicant's data is summarized in this section.
L703: In the first 16 weeks of treatment, more subjects (22.1%) in the risankizumab 150 mg group reported infection AEs (90.8 E/100 PY) compared to 14.7% of subjects in the placebo group (56.5 E/100 PY). Infection rates appear to be comparable to the infection rates seen in the active control groups. The most common infection AEs in the risankizumab 150 mg group (\ge 1% of subjects) were viral upper respiratory tract infection, upper respiratory tract infection, urinary tract infection, and sinusitis.
L704: Over the entire psoriasis program, 40.8% of subjects in the All Risankizumab group reported infection AEs (75.5 E/100 PY). The majority of infections (97.8%) were nonserious.
L705: ## Serious
L706: In the first 16 weeks of treatment, 5 (0.4%) subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg group experienced a total of 7 serious infections (1.7 E/100 PY) compared to 1 (0.3%) of the subjects in the placebo group (1.1 E/100 PY). The risankizumab associated serious infections included cellulitis (2 subjects), herpes zoster, osteomyelitis, sepsis, perirectal abscess and diverticulitis. Cellulitis progressed to sepsis in one subject and to osteomyelitis in another subject. Both subjects had a history of cellulitis and the latter also had a history of osteomyelitis and pre-existing foot ulceration. No risankizumab-treated subjects discontinued study drug due to serious infections.
L707: After 52 weeks of exposure, 8 (1.3%) subjects in the risankizumab group experienced a total of 11 serious infections (1.8 E/100 PY), which was comparable to the active control group. This includes the 7 serious infections listed above plus an additional case of pneumonia, an additional case of sepsis, pyelonephritis, and gastroenteritis.
L708: Over the entire psoriasis program, 39 serious infections were reported in 30 (1.3%) subjects treated with risankizumab (1.7 E/100 PY). Serious infections reported in \ge 3 subjects included sepsis (7), cellulitis (5), and pneumonia (4); the rest of the serious infections were reported in 2 or fewer subjects. Two subjects discontinued due to serious infections (1 due to sepsis and pneumonia and 1 due to pneumonia).
L709: ## Opportunistic and Infections of Interest
L710: In the pivotal Phase 3 studies, subjects with active tuberculosis (TB), HIV or viral hepatitis were excluded; subjects with latent TB were required to start treatment prophylaxis prior to starting on risankizumab. There were no AEs of active or latent TB reported in any treatment arm in the first 16 weeks of treatment. One subject in the risankizumab 150 mg group reported a herpes zoster, which was an SAE, but did not result in discontinuation of treatment. Twenty (1.5%) subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg group experienced fungal infections (5.2 E/100 PY) compared to 1 (0.3%) of the subjects in the placebo group (1.1 E/100PY). Fungal infection rates in the risankizumab group appear to fall within the rates seen in the active control groups. Fungal infections in the risankizumab group were primarily of a type of tinea infection. Mucocutaneous candida reported in the risankizumab group included 1 case each of balanitis and skin candidiasis. There was 1 case of fungal laryngitis. None of the fungal infections were serious or led to discontinuation of study drug.
L711: After 52 weeks of exposure, there were no AEs of active TB reported in any treatment arm. There was 1 case each of latent TB and mycobacterium tuberculosis complex test positive reported in the risankizumab group. There were no new cases of herpes zoster; 1 additional case of mucocutaneous candidiasis (oral candidiasis) was reported. Twenty-four (4.0%) subjects in the risankizumab group experienced a total of 26 fungal infections (4.2 E/100 PY) which was higher than the active comparator in which 2 (1.0%) subjects (1.0 E/ 100 PY) reported fungal infections.
L712: Over the entire psoriasis program, the rates of opportunistic infections among risankizumab-treated subjects with psoriasis were low (0.3 E/100 PY). None of the opportunistic infections were serious or led to discontinuation of study drug. Opportunistic infections include CMV reactivation and esophageal candidiasis (1 subject each) and oral candidiasis (4 subjects).
L713: Herpes zoster was reported in 7 subjects. Two events were considered serious, but do not appear to be multidermatomal from the information provided. Neither subject discontinued study drug due to herpes zoster. Latent TB was reported in 7 subjects and 2 subjects reported a positive test for Mycobacterium tuberculosis complex. A total of 64 (2.9%) subjects (3.4 E/100 PY) treated with risankizumab reported fungal infections.
L714: The additional information provided in the safety update showed that the rate and pattern of overall infections, serious infections and opportunistic and fungal infections remained generally unchanged. There continued to be no cases of active TB.
L715: The following language is recommended to be included in product labeling:
L716: In the first 16 weeks, infections occurred in 22.1% of the TRADENAME group (90.8 events per 100 subject-years) compared to 14.7% of the placebo group (56.5 events per 100 subject-years. Five (0.4%) subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg group experienced a total of 7 serious infections (1.7 E/100 PY) compared to 1 (0.3%) of the subjects in the placebo group (1.1 E/100 PY).
L717: ## **Malignancies**
L718: The Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials excluded subjects with active or suspected malignancy or history of malignancy within 5 years prior to screening, except appropriately treated basal or squamous cell carcinoma of the skin or in situ carcinoma of uterine cervix.
L719: Due to immunosuppressive properties, the risk of malignancy associated with risankizumab use is a potential concern. Data was evaluated at 16 weeks, 52 weeks and with all data to date including some subjects (85) with up to 3 years of exposure. The review identified some cases which were not included in the applicant's summary or tables. In the narrative summaries, the subject number for these cases are marked with an asterisk (*).
L720: In the first 16 weeks of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 psoriasis studies (primary safety pool), 3 (0.2%) risankizumab subjects reported non-NMSC malignant tumor AEs (invasive lobular breast carcinoma, malignant melanoma in situ, and esophageal carcinoma. An additional three subjects reported NMSC (2 with squamous cell carcinoma of skin and 1 with basal cell carcinoma). In the placebo group, there were no non-NMSC malignancies, 1 subject reported squamous cell carcinoma of skin. For the active comparators, no malignant tumors were reported for ustekinumab and 1 (0.3%) malignancy (gallbladder cancer) was reported for adalimumab.
L721: After 52 weeks of exposure (ustekinumab-controlled analysis set), additional malignancies were reported in 3 subjects in the risankizumab group. All were NMSCs (2 basal cell carcinoma and 1 squamous cell carcinoma of the skin). The event rate for non-NMSC malignancies was 0.5 E/100 PY. In the active comparator group, there was 1 (0.5%) reported malignancy for prostate cancer.
L722: The All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set includes subjects that received a dose of risankizumab who are followed through to the end of exposure (including ongoing LTE studies). In this analysis set, there were 30 reports of malignant tumors, 19 of which were NMSC. A summary of malignancy AEs is shown in Table 27. Of the 11 non-NMSC malignancies, the most frequent non-NMSC malignant tumors were breast cancer in 4 subjects (events of breast cancer, invasive ductal breast carcinoma, and invasive lobular breast carcinoma) and malignant melanoma in situ and prostate cancer that occurred in 2 subjects each. These cases along with additional cases submitted in the safety update are summarized below.

L740: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L741: Breast cancer is the most common cancer in the US followed by lung cancer and prostate cancer and the incidence of melanoma of the skin has been rising.
L742: For the overall development program, malignancy rate with risankizumab treatment was 1.4 E/100 PY. The malignancy rate excluding NMSC was 0.62 E/100 PY.
L743: The event rate for risankizumab is increased, but not substantially, as compared to other sources of malignancy data in psoriasis patients. The rates of malignant tumors (excluding NMSC) reported in the ustekinumab, ixekizumab, secukinumab, and guselkumab clinical development programs ranged from 0.31 – 0.49 E/100 PY. The rate of first malignancy among patients who did not have a history of malignancy (excluding NMSC) reported for biologics in the Psoriasis Longitudinal Assessment and Registry14 (total PYs, 46,041) was 0.55 per 100 PYs (95% CI, 0.48-0.62).
L744: Although the event rate for malignancy is slightly higher than comparable data in psoriasis patients, there was only one death from malignancy in the risankizumab development program. At this juncture a causal link between the product and malignancy is not evident. Additional long-term data is needed.
L745: Skin cancer is the most common class of human malignancies and the elevated risk of skin cancers in chronically immunosuppressed individuals is well-established from studies in organ transplant patients. The ratio of SCC:BCC is 0.2:1.0 in nontransplant patients. After transplant, the ratio changes. The incidence of SCC increases greatly, but BCC increases only slightly, resulting in a ratio of SCC:BCC of 1.8:1.015.
L746: Of the 25 NMSC malignancies reported in subjects on risankizumab, the most common were basal cell carcinoma (15 events) and Bowen's disease/ squamous cell combined included 10 events (ratio of 1:1.5). While the ratio is narrower than that seen in nontransplant patients, it does not invert due to an increase of SCC, suggesting less of an immunosuppressive affect than seen in transplant patients.
L747: There is a concern that this new biologic product may increase the risk of malignancies due to its immunosuppressive effect. The limited duration of observation during the drug development program does not allow detection of rare events with a long latency periods such as malignancy. Therefore, postmarketing data are needed to evaluate the long-term risk of malignancy in patients with psoriasis receiving risankizumab. A PMR is recommended for an observational study to collect additional data on long-term safety and evaluate the occurrence of long-latency safety outcomes, including malignancy that cannot be adequately assessed in the clinical trial program.
L748: ## **Cardiovascular (CV) events**
L749: 14 Fiorentino et al. Risk of malignancy with systemic psoriasis treatment in the Psoriasis Longitudinal Assessment Registry J Am Acad Dermatol Nov 2017: 845-854.
L750: 15 Penn I. Skin disorders in organ transplant recipients: external anogenital lesions. Arch Dermatol 1997;133:221-223.
L751: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L752: Based on epidemiologic associations between psoriasis and Cardiovascular (CV) events, and the potential association between anti-cytokine therapies used in the treatment of moderate to severe psoriasis and CV events, the applicant conducted additional analyses on adjudicated major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE), extended MACE, and other CV events. MACE was defined as the composite of CV death, nonfatal myocardial infarction, and nonfatal stroke. Extended MACE was defined as the composite of MACE plus hospitalization for unstable angina and coronary revascularization procedures. Other CV events were defined as thrombotic events, cardiac arrhythmia, and congestive heart failure.
L753: Baseline cardiovascular risk factors and baseline history of cardiovascular disease were evenly distributed among the risankizumab arms and control arms (ustekinumab, adalimumab, and placebo) of the studies comprising the primary safety pool.
L754: The review team has noted that in the 52-week Ustekinumab-controlled safety analysis set, there is an imbalance of cardiac Serious Adverse Events (SAEs). Eight (1.3%) subjects in the risankizumab group had SAEs in the cardiac disorder SOC compared to 1 (0.5%) subject in the ustekinumab group. The imbalance seems to be primarily due to increased events reported as preferred terms “congestive heart failure” and “cardiac arrhythmia”.
L755: Fred Senatore, MD, PhD, FDA Division of Cardiovascular and Renal Products, reviewed the CV adverse event data from the clinical development program.
L756: Cardiovascular events in the primary safety pool dataset are shown in Table 29. The numbers of events were low and comparable across all arms: placebo (1.7%), ustekinumab (1.7%), adalimumab (1.3%), risankizumab 90 mg (2.4%), risankizumab 150 mg (0.7%), and risankizumab 150-180 mg (0.7%). The estimated annualized rates of CV events were: placebo (5%), ustekinumab (5%), adalimumab (4%), risankizumab 90 mg (7%), risankizumab 150 mg (2%), and risankizumab 150-180 mg (2%).

There were no reported events in the ustekinumab arms. The events in the risankizumab 150 mg arms (efficacious dose) were low: MACE (0.3%), extended MACE (0.7%) and “other events” that included cardiac arrhythmia (0.7%) and CHF (0.7%).

L770: The results of a search of the case report forms detailing the adjudicated heart failure and arrhythmia events leading to the apparent imbalance between ustekinumab and risankizumab are shown in Table 31. In the 4 cases of CHF, the verbatim reports were CHF, left cardiac decompensation (each required hospitalization), mitral valve incompetence and acute kidney failure superimposed on chronic kidney disease (each of these 2 required an urgent visit). There were 5 cases of supraventricular arrhythmia (SVA) none of which had ischemia. One of the cases was deemed a pre-existing condition and was not counted likely for that reason. Of the 4 remaining SVA events that were counted, the verbatim reports were atrial flutter with 4:1 AVB, “tachycardia”, atrial fibrillation, and AV nodal reentrant tachycardia. All were described as new onset. Subject (b) (6) had coronary bypass grafting reported as an adverse event one month earlier that may have been a confounder in the SVA event. The other cases of SVA did not have reported cardiac histories suggestive of an arrhythmic risk. In the absence of imbalances in the larger sample size of the primary pooled safety population, this low-incidence observation was likely due to chance.

L777: ## Anaphylaxis and hypersensitivity
L778: As an immunoglobulin protein, subcutaneous administration of risankizumab may be associated with immunogenicity and/or hypersensitivity reactions. To identify subjects who experienced serious hypersensitivity reactions, the applicant conducted a search using the hypersensitivity (narrow) SMQ and the anaphylactic reaction (narrow) SMQ.
L779: In the first 16 weeks of treatment a slightly greater percent of subjects (2.9%) in the risankizumab treatment group compared to placebo (2.3%) reported hypersensitivity AEs. The percent of reactions seen with risankizumab was within the range of the active controls. The majority of reported hypersensitivity AE associated with risankizumab treatment were skin reactions (dermatitis, contact dermatitis, rash, eczema, and skin reaction). The most frequently reported hypersensitivity AE was eczema, which was reported in 11 (0.8%) of the risankizumab-treated subjects and 1 (0.3%) placebo-treated subjects. Urticaria was reported associated with risankizumab treatment in 6 subjects (0.5%) compared to 1 subject (0.3%) in the placebo group. Urticaria reactions seen in the risankizumab group was within the range of those seen in the active control groups. None of the hypersensitivity reactions were serious or led to study drug discontinuation. There were no reported cases of anaphylaxis identified reaction in any of the treatment groups.
L780: 16 Lebwohl, M, 2012, Interleukin 12/23 agents and major adverse cardiac events, Archives of Dermatology, 148 (11): 1329,
https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamadermatology/fullarticle/1392590
L781: After 52 weeks of exposure, 37 subjects (6.2%) in the risankizumab group experienced a hypersensitivity reaction AE (7.9 E/100 PY). There was no change in the general profile of reported PTs and the majority describe dermatitis. None of the hypersensitivity reactions were serious or led to study drug discontinuation. There were no reported cases of anaphylaxis identified reaction in any of the treatment groups.
L782: Over the entire psoriasis program, 137 (6.1%) subjects reported 176 hypersensitivity reaction AEs (8.1 E/100 PY). There was 1 case of erythema multiforme that was considered an SAE and was confounded by concomitant antibiotic therapy. One subject discontinued study drug due to an AE of dermatitis. There was no report of anaphylactic reaction.
L783: The additional information provided in the safety update showed that the rate and pattern of overall hypersensitivity reactions remained generally unchanged. There was no report of anaphylactic reaction.
L784: The applicant conducted a comprehensive review of serious hypersensitivity events regardless of dose or indication. Two cases of anaphylactic reaction have been identified in subjects treated with risankizumab in the other indication studies. These 2 cases are described below:
L785: In the open label Crohn's disease study, [M15-989 (1311.20)], **Subject** (b) (6) a 43-year-old white female had an anaphylactic reaction on Day 175 and was hospitalized. The applicant provides the following: "Within minutes of the injection, given on the same day, she became clammy and tachycardic. The subject did not lose consciousness but had chest tightness. Her heart rate was 130 bpm and systolic blood pressure was 140 mmHg." It appears the reaction is temporally related to intravenous ferinject. The relationship of the reaction to the study drug is not clear based on the information provided. Treatment medications included adrenaline, hydrocortisone, and chlorpheniramine. Within 2 hours of onset, the subject recovered. Study drug was not interrupted due to this event. The subject had been exposed to previous iron injections without any known reactions. On Day 575 the subject had a ferinject infusion without incident. Both ferinject and a risankizumab injection were given on the day of the reaction. There appears to be a negative rechallenge with both ferinject and risankizumab. There have been reports of reactions associated with iron infusion. However, the contribution of study drug cannot be determined.
L786: In the psoriatic arthritis study, [M16-002 (1311.5)], **Subject** (b) (6) a 50-year-old white female reported an anaphylactic reaction. The subject was biologic naïve and 1 hour after the first dose of 150 mg of risankizumab, the subject experienced symptoms of increasing dizziness, weakness, nausea, a macular rash on the thorax (décolleté) and neck, and dyspnea. The subject's blood pressure decreased from a pre-dose measurement of 160/90 to a nadir of 90/60,
L787: 70 minutes post dose. The blood pressure increased to 140/90, recorded as supine, 140 minutes post dose. Tachycardia was not recorded.
L788: The subject reported experiencing anxiety prior to the injection. There was no history of facial swelling, mucosal involvement, pruritus, or urticaria. The subject was transported to the hospital. Treatment medications included intravenous electrolytes, ionosteril, fenistil, ranitidine and prednisolone. The subject's condition improved, and she was discharged from the hospital the following day. The drug was permanently discontinued. The event was considered by the investigator, but not the sponsor to have a reasonable possibility of relationship to study drug. While the temporal relationship tends to favor a possible anaphylaxis event, other provided details seem to be more consistent with a possible vasovagal episode. There is insufficient information to attribute this event to an IgE-mediated anaphylactic reaction.
L789: There does not appear to sufficient information to include anaphylactic reactions to section 4 product labeling. Cases of urticaria and edema were reported, but none of the reactions were serious or led to discontinuation. The applicant did not propose hypersensitivity language in section 5 of product labeling. The team discussed including a class warning for hypersensitivity, but opted to not include at this juncture due to the lack of serious cases seen in the clinical trials. It is recommended that the applicant receives a PMR for post-marketing observational study which includes an assessment for hypersensitivity reactions.
L790: ## **Injection-site reaction (ISR)**
L791: In the Phase 3 clinical studies, the applicant used a Local Tolerability Case Report Form, which characterized the subject-reported injection site reactions from the previous dose administration. This form was used to summarize injection-site reactions in the Placebo-Controlled and Ustekinumab-Controlled Analysis Sets. The form assessed swelling, induration, heat, redness, pain and “other.” Redness was more frequently reported as occurring in the risankizumab group as compared to placebo. Overall, reports of injection site reaction symptoms were generally low.
L792: Because the Local Tolerability case report form (CRF) was not employed across all studies in the psoriasis program, a search for AEs using the Injection Site Reaction CMQ was performed by the applicant for the Primary Safety Pool and the All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set. In both analysis sets, none of the injection site reactions were considered severe or led to study discontinuation.
L793: In the Primary Safety Pool, the proportion of subjects in the risankizumab 150 mg group who had injection site reaction AEs was higher than placebo (1.5% vs 1.0%, respectively), but lower than with ustekinumab (3.8%) or adalimumab (5.6%). The most frequently reported injection site-related AEs associated with risankizumab were injection site erythema in 10 (0.7%) subjects and injection site hemorrhage, injection site pain, and injection site pruritus in 3 (0.2%) subjects each.
L794: In the All Risankizumab Psoriasis Analysis Set, injection site reactions were reported for 69 subjects (3.1%), with the most frequently reported injection site related AEs (&gt; 2 subjects) being injection-site erythema in 33 (1.5%) subjects; injection site pain, injection site pruritus, and injection site reaction in 8 (0.4%) subjects each; injection site haematoma and injection site swelling in 7 (0.3%) subjects each; injection site haemorrhage in 6 (0.3%) subjects; and injection site bruising in 3 (0.1%) subjects.
L795: Reported preferred terms that describe injection site reactions (injection site bruising, erythema, extravasation, hematoma, hemorrhage, infection, inflammation, irritation, pain, pruritus, reaction, swelling, warmth) were pooled for labeling.
L796: ## **Neuropsychiatric events**
L797: Multiple studies have indicated that patients with psoriasis have a greater risk of the development of depression and suicide ideation than the general population. To address the concern that anticytokine therapies may potentiate this risk, depression and suicidal ideation and behavior (SIB) AEs were identified through searches of the MedDRA depression (excluding suicide and self-injury) SMQ and the suicide/self-injury (narrow) SMQ.
L798: The entry criteria for the risankizumab clinical studies did not exclude subjects with a history of SIB. In the first 16 weeks of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 psoriasis studies (primary safety pool), 4 (0.3%) subjects in the risankizumab group had a depression-related event and 2 subjects (0.2%) reported SIB (suicide ideation and suicide attempt). One subject who had a SAE of depression also had an SAE of SIB. None of the depression events led to discontinuation of study drug.
L799: One event of suicide attempt was reported in the adalimumab group. There was 1 subject each, reporting suicidal ideation (SI) in the placebo group and the ustekinumab group. This ustekinumab-treated SI event led to discontinuation of study drug. There were no other discontinuations due to SIB AEs.
L800: In the overall development program (All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set), 22 (1.0%) subjects had a depression event. For 2 subjects these events were an SAE. None of the depression events led to discontinuation of study drug. There were no completed suicides in the risankizumab psoriasis clinical studies. There were no additional SIB events with prolonged exposure.
L801: Both risankizumab-treated subject with SIB events had past medical histories of psychiatric illness:
L802: - Subject (b) (6) (Study M16-010), a 55-year-old male with a prior history of bipolar disorder, depression, schizophrenia, and suicidal ideation, experienced an event of suicide attempt and worsening depression on Day 3. Study drug was not discontinued and both events were considered resolved on Day 11.
- Subject (b) (6) (Study M15-992 [1311.4]), a 33-year-old female with a history of bipolar disorder and depression who experienced a nonserious event of
L803: bipolar-self harm thoughts (coded to self-injurious ideation) on Day 54. Study drug was not discontinued.
L804: With the safety update, 6 new subjects experienced a depression event [28 subjects in all (1.1%; 0.9 E/100 PY)]. None of the depression events led to discontinuation of study drug. There were no new cases of suicidal ideation and behavior (SIB) in the safety update.
L805: Based on the clinical trial data available, there is insufficient evidence of increased risk of SIB or other psychiatric issues thus far with risankizumab, in terms of direct drug-related effect. Suicide and self-harm risks are complex behaviors which are influenced by many social, psychological and environmental factors. To better understand the impact, if any, treatment with risankizumab may have, postmarketing data are needed to evaluate the long-term risk in patients with psoriasis receiving risankizumab. A PMR is recommended for an observational study to collect additional data.
L806: ## **Hepatic events**
L807: The event rates of hepatic AEs observed at Week 16 (primary safety pool) occurred in 20 (1.5%) subjects in the risankizumab group who experienced a total of 24 hepatic AEs (6.0 E/100 PY) compared to 6 (2.0%) in the placebo group (7.6 E/100 PY), 5 (2.1%) subjects in the ustekinumab group (9.2 E/100 PY), and 3 (1.0%) subjects in the adalimumab group (5.3 E/100 PY). The majority of the hepatic AEs were related to liver enzyme abnormalities. Other events reported that were not related to liver injury/increased transaminase(s) were abnormal ultrasound of the liver (1 event) and hepatic steatosis (4 events).
L808: Two subjects treated with risankizumab and concomitant isoniazid had AEs that were considered serious and subjects discontinued treatment. Two subjects in ustekinumab treatment group and 1 subject in the placebo group also reported drug-induced liver injury/liver injury SAEs for event rates of (1.1 E/100 PY) and (2.6 E/100 PY), respectively compared to the risankizumab group (0.5E/100 PY).
L809: Other notable hepatic AEs included: LFT abnormal (Grade 4) and LFT increased (Grade 3). These events were not SAEs and did not lead to discontinuation of study drug. The cases are described below:
L810: **Subject** (b)(6) in Study M16-008 [1311.3] is a 62-year-old Asian male with a history of alcohol use (2 drinks per week), latent TB, 21-day treatment with isoniazid (INH) prior to study who was treated with risankizumab for chronic plaque psoriasis. Baseline lab values showed a slightly elevated total bilirubin (TBi) and gamma glutamyl transpeptidase (GGT). The subject received 1 dose of risankizumab and discontinued from the study. The reason for which is not clear in the applicant's provided narrative. On Day 20 (Post-Treatment Day 19) the subject experienced drug-induced hepatitis requiring hospitalization. Alanine amino transferase (ALT), aspartate amino transferase (AST) and GGT values
L811: were all &gt; 20 \times upper limit of normal (ULN); alkaline phosphatase (ALP) was 2X ULN and TBi was within normal limits (WNL).
L812: INH was discontinued. On Day 92 (Post-Treatment Day 91) laboratory values (AST, ALT, ALP) were within normal laboratory reference range. Laboratory values are shown in Table 32.

L816: Both the investigator and the sponsor considered the event of drug-induced liver injury as having a reasonable possibility of being related to study drug. The case is confounded by treatment with INH for latent TB. INH is associated with both mild hepatotoxicity and hepatitis, sometimes advancing to fulminant liver failure. The duration of INH treatment prior to onset of liver toxicity has been reported to be between 1-25 weeks duration, with an average duration of 12 weeks. It cannot be determined the degree, if any risankizumab contributed to the reported AE of drug-induced hepatitis.
L817: **Subject** (b) (6) in Study M16-010 [1311.30] is a 59-year-old, white male with a history of obesity, latent TB, 68-day treatment with isoniazid (INH) prior to study, possible daily use of acetaminophen who was treated with risankizumab for chronic plaque psoriasis. Baseline lab values showed a slightly GGT and elevated AST (5X ULN). On Day 30 (Post-Treatment Day 1) the subject experienced hepatic injury. ALT and AST increased to about 12X ULN; ALP and TBi remained WNL.
L818: Risankizumab and INH were discontinued. On Day 138 (Post-Treatment Day 109) laboratory values (AST, ALT) were within normal laboratory reference range. Laboratory values are shown in Table 33.

The subject continued the trial as planned and recovered by Day 92.
L825: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761105
## (Skyrizi, Risankizumab)
L826: After 52 weeks of exposure, the hepatic event rate in the risankizumab group was 6.5 E/100 PY. Twenty-eight (4.7%) subjects in the risankizumab group experienced 40 hepatic events compared with 10 (5.0%) subjects having 13 events in the ustekinumab group (6.4 E/100 PY). Compared to the 16-week analysis, the 52-week event rate for risankizumab increased slightly, but the event rate for ustekinumab decreased to a rate comparable to risankizumab.
L827: In the All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set, hepatic AEs were reported in 77 (3.4%) subjects treated with risankizumab (121 events, 5.6 E/100 PY). Of these 2234 subjects, there were 12 cases reporting abnormal transaminases (AST increased, ALT increased, LFT increased, GGT increased, LFT abnormal) that were RCTC Grade \ge 3 (&gt; 3 \times ULN). Of these, 3 cases were \ge 20 \times ULN elevation (these include the cases described above). None of the subjects with severe hepatic AEs had bilirubin of Grade 2 or above in severity. One subject (b)(6) with an AE of congestive heart failure had transaminases (&gt; 3 \times ULN) and elevated TBI (&gt; 2 \times ULN), however ALP was elevated, and the elevations were likely due to cholestasis. One subject with ALT increased and 1 subject with ALT/AST increased discontinued study drug due to their AEs
L828: In the Phase 2 psoriatic arthritis study, Study M16-002, 1 subject (b)(6) a 57-year-old white male with a history of alcohol use, fatty liver, hypertriglyceridemia, hypercholesterolemia (on atorvastatin), pancreatitis, and cardiomyopathy met laboratory criteria for Hy's with Grade 3 increases in ALT, AST and bilirubin 56 days after the last dose of study drug (Day 170). Steatohepatitis was confirmed by biopsy. The subject was not receiving concomitant methotrexate. Multiple confounders included alcohol abuse (about 1 – 2 bottles of wine per day for past month), baseline increased transaminases (GGT, AST, ALT), and history of fatty liver.
L829: In the All Risankizumab – Psoriasis Analysis Set, shifts in baseline chemistry values of grade 0 to 2 at Baseline to grade 3 to 4 at any time during the study occurred in 16 subjects for ALT, 14 subjects for AST, 27 subjects for GGT.
L830: In the safety update, 112 (4.5%) subjects (183 events, 5.4 E/100 PY) reported hepatic AEs. The updated included one case of autoimmune hepatitis (subject (b)(6) in study M15-997). The subject is a 54-year old white female with a history of dyslipidemia, hypothyroidism and coronary heart disease, and elevated ALT at baseline (ALT 43; ULN 34 U/L). After treatment with risankizumab for approximately 1 year the subject experienced elevated for AST, ALT (both &gt; 20 \times ULN), total bilirubin (&gt; 3 \times ULN), ALP (&gt; 2 \times ULN), lactate dehydrogenase, and GGT and was diagnosed with autoimmune hepatitis based on positive autoimmune serologies and biopsy of the liver. The subject was started on a prednisone with improvement in symptoms and subsequent normalization of her liver tests. Study drug was discontinued.
L831: Overall, 6 subjects (0.2%) discontinued treatment due to hepatic events. Some serious hepatic events occurred. There were no cases of fulminant hepatic failure. Most of the events were confounded with concomitant medication or comorbid conditions. It is possible that risankizumab may exacerbate preexisting conditions, but this has not been
L832: seen with other biologic therapies in the treatment of psoriasis. To better understand the impact, if any, treatment with risankizumab may have, postmarketing data are needed to evaluate the long-term risk in patients with psoriasis receiving risankizumab. A PMR is recommended for an observational study to collect additional data.
L833: ## 7.3.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L834: Five subgroup categories for intrinsic factors were analyzed to assess if there was any impact of these factors on the overall safety profile of risankizumab. Subgroups included race (White, Black or African American, Other), gender (male, female), age (&lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years), Baseline static Physician's Global Assessment (sPGA; 3, 4), and body weight (≤ 100 kg, ≥ 100 kg).
L835: There are differences in enrollment based on demographics. The risankizumab psoriasis clinical study population was predominantly white (78%) and male (70%). Overall, baseline sPGA score was moderate (3) in approximately 80% of subjects. Approximately 90% of subjects were between under the age of 65.
L836: All 5 deaths in subjects treated with risankizumab occurred in male subjects, which made up the majority of trial subjects by gender. Differences were noted in the rates of SAEs (11% vs. 5.6%), AEs leading to discontinuation (2.1% vs. 1.5%), severe AEs (8.2% vs. 5.4%) and deaths(0.4% vs. 0.2%), which were higher in the subjects ≥65 as compared to &lt;65 years.
L837: Generally, there are no substantial differences in the risk of adverse reactions in demographic subgroups to warrant restricted use. However, because the trials were not powered for these analyses, the data must be interpreted with caution.
L838: ## 7.3.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L839: At the time of submission, 2 open-label long-term studies [M15-997 and M16-009 (1311.13)] and 1 random withdrawal study [M15-992 (1311.4)] were ongoing. Safety analysis sets included interim data. These studies are described in section 7.1 of this review.
L840: With the August 16, 2018 submission of the 120 day safety update report, the applicant provided additional exposure data from subjects who initiated risankizumab since the initial submission, as well as continued follow-up data from Studies M15-992 (1311.4), M15-997, and M16-009 (1311.13). The applicant also provided new data from Studies M16-178 and M16-004 (1311.38).
